  11 – 7 = 4  and  4 × 6 = 24.

Now we still must “use” the extra 2. One way to do this without changing the value is to multiply by 2/2 (which equals 1). That is, we can write

  (11 – 7) × 6 × (2/2).

Step by step:
1. Compute 11 – 7 = 4.
2. Then 4 × 6 = 24.
3. Finally, multiplying by 2/2 (which is 1) leaves the result unchanged: 24 × 1 = 24.

Thus, the expression

  (11 – 7) × 6 × (2/2)

uses each of the four numbers exactly once and evaluates exactly to 24.

The final answer is:  \boxed{(11-7)\times 6\times \frac{2}{2}}.
